ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e4880	Mr. Vice-Chancellor, please.

I believe that this measure will, in total, increase security, as shown by experience first in Scandinavian countries, but now also in southern countries. Because the question was also discussed whether to do it only in the winter months or whether to do it only on high-level roads or on free land roads, municipal roads: I think it should be carried out uniformly for all vehicles, as it is now being discussed. The vehicle industry – I am convinced of this – will also quickly adjust to it and develop special light sources, so that I can answer your question in such a way that the loss of safety in single-track vehicles, which are already on the move with an on-line light, will be small, but the overall increase in road safety can be enormous. Here too, there are calculations that have been examined by experts and confirmed that the accident figures can be reduced by up to 15 percent in total. I think that's worth the try all the time.

You will not be surprised, I hope that on my last visit to the United States, I have looked at the technology and how it is already implemented in practice, in order to take good suggestions, ideas, innovations with you to Europe, to Austria, in order to be able to move towards more environmentally friendly vehicles in transport. Individual measures can always be discussed. These costs of course also cost money, which must also be agreed with the Minister of Finance. You don't always agree on the effectiveness. Also, environmental groupings are not always convinced that the individual measure is correct. I therefore believe that we are correct in addressing the problem by trying to force more environmentally friendly impulses on the field of research and innovation.

So for more than 40 years there has been the possibility of a mutual blockade in this house, but that does not mean that it would have always been exploited, but at least the possibility has passed. Ladies and gentlemen! In the new federal constitution concerning the school sector, we have secured religious education. We have secured the relationship between school and church. We have laid down compulsory schooling and compulsory vocational education as an essential point in the Austrian educational landscape. In particular, I invite the media to look at the paper a little more precisely and take from an extensive paragraph not only the last half-rate and derive from it any interpretation. This makes it quite clear to us, as a legislator, that many reforms will be made in the future, that many new things will be made possible, but that what is good in the Austrian education system – I see this in connection with today's § 3 of the School Organisation Act – will remain secure. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We have managed to give security on the one hand and on the other hand to ensure openness for new, increased flexibility in the interior of the Austrian school. Point Report of the Transport Committee on the Government template (814, e.g._Other Organiser ==References====External links==* Official website January 1976 on the competence of the authorities and the aggressment of administrative evasions in maritime matters on the Lake Constance and on the amendment of the Shipping Police Act and the Maritime Act (Shipping Law 2005) (907 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e11340	We're now on the 2nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker was Mr Böhm. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e11369	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Since the adoption of the Shipping Act in 1997, administrative practice and progressive developments, such as in the field of European integration and communication technologies, have led to the need for renewal and adaptation of various legislation. The aim of this amendment is to adapt Austrian shipping law to existing legal standards, such as those of the European Union. At its meeting on 28 October, the Transport Committee adopted the text of the present proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to turnover taxes and on the approximation of the laws of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. Negotiating on April 2005, and the draft law contained in the Government bill was adopted unanimously during the vote. According to Statistics Austria, the transport volume on the road is around 285 million tonnes, on the Danube about 12 million tonnes, on the rail around 84 million tonnes. That is, it corresponds to the transport on the Danube 4 percent of the total transport of the road and 14 percent of that on the rail. Large quantities of various solid and liquid substances are still being transported through Austria via the water. This leads to two areas of concern: safety and the environment. First of all, on the environment: The problem here is the folding of board's unrat in water. Again and again oily water from tank tanks and above all bilge water in the water is disposed of. The new legislation here calls on ports to set up appropriate disposal facilities. The aim is to prevent, on the one hand, vehicles from disposing of unattended quantities of oil residues or oily water at no additional cost and, on the other hand, vehicles entering the port exclusively for the purpose of the disposal of the disposal performance, without facing a significant port fee. This will force the use of de-oiler ships. Secondly, safety is mainly concerned with navigation, weather information and water levels and with improving telecommunications through the River Information System. It is also about balancing radio systems at a common European level. In the interests of security, I would ask for a general agreement.

We can see how far the authorities worked at the time. But the development in air transport continues. We know that we are expected to have 20 million passengers in Vienna-Schwechat in 2010, at the latest in 2012. If this is achieved, the noise limitations imposed will have to be amended accordingly. We have a long-standing mediation procedure behind us at Vienna-Schwechat Airport, with over 50 parties, with citizens' initiatives, with representatives of Vienna districts, with Lower Austrian mayors et cetera. The following are defined: The mediation procedure will first end, and then the EIA procedure will be initiated for the third runway. The result is practically complete. It is carried by almost everyone and includes: comprehensive night flight bans and the relocation of flight routes as far as possible over uninhabited territory. Claims to move Vienna-Schwechat Airport at all, for example to Marchfeld or even Hungary, would result in huge investments being devalued and countless jobs lost. The demands that only flights and departures to Vienna-Schwechat should come and not be reversed are also, I must say, just too naïve, because then many flights would be lost and therefore the economic location of Austria would be seriously threatened. Also, the desire for only more Chapter 4 aircraft to land cannot be taken into account, with only Chapter 3 aircraft currently in Vienna-Swechat being allowed to land. The Chapter 4 aircraft will only be allowed on a regular basis, which will take some time. In this sense, I would like to ask for the approval of the implementation of the EU Directive, and also of our colleagues from the Greens, for a somewhat more realistic view of aviation.

I too can speak about this agreement and welcome the fact that under this agreement there is a need to facilitate the implementation of ambulance and rescue flights. Since travel between Austria and Slovenia is becoming increasingly strong and there is increasing alpine tourism, it is essential that we make it very easy for injured or ill Austrian citizens, but also Slovenian citizens, to catch up on the airway. To date, cross-border rescue and ambulance flights have been subject to major administrative conditions. Since the time factor is very important in the event of accidents and it is often about minutes to quickly store people at risk of life, this agreement reduces administrative formalities to a minimum. The present draft provides for the facilitation of customs and border police procedures: the exemption from customs force, the removal from border police clearance, the widespread reduction of the necessary customs formalities and the simplification of the procedure in the delivery of the flight plan. Ladies and gentlemen! In the interests of security, this agreement is necessary after it has already been agreed with several neighbouring countries. It is a real gap that we are closing today, and I am pleased that all the parties agree to this proposal.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e14560	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I would like to make a few general remarks on the subject of this urgent question. The Second Republic of Austria, 60. We celebrated a few days ago, from the very beginning it was an antithesis of totalitarianism and of every kind of dictatorship. The democratic foundation on which all social groups in our country are professed is beyond doubt and is also the basic consensus of this democracy. This also includes the fact that, especially in the last two decades, we have become more aware of the fact that many Austrians were actively involved in the crimes of National Socialism and that after 1945, numerous legal measures tried to make up for national socialist injustice, as far as possible and is possible. But I don't hide: It has not been done enough in all areas. That is why, in a far-reaching consensus of all the key political forces in this country since the 1990s, a bundle of measures have been developed to set effective signals of legal nature and to make possible material achievements by the Republic where this was necessary. Here in Parliament a first important step has been taken by the National Fund of the Republic of Austria for victims of National Socialism. Other measures have also been adopted by the Federal Government since 2000. In this context, I would like to remind you of the rules in the field of forced labour. Last day, Hugo Portisch's documentation on Austrian television gave an objective and unwarranted witness about what has been done here. Ladies and gentlemen! In view of this, before addressing the questions in detail, I may appeal to You are right: Let us discuss the political issues in connection with our recent history, not in the spirit of party political polemics, but state-political responsibility and find common solutions! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig: Where's your responsibility? All this is on your note! You're reading!]] I now come to answer your urgent questions. Questions 1 to 3: In general, I have the impression that all the key political forces in the Federal Government and in the countries, especially the President of the Federal Government, the Federal Government, as well as all the Heads of State, have tried to use the 2005 year of thought to reflect honestly on the events at that time and on the worthy memory, which, as I believe, has succeeded in an excellent way. Federal Councillor Kampl has resigned from his function. [[Abg. Dr. Grünewald: But not because of you!]] I mean, that was the right consequence. ==References== In his speech in the context of the State Act, Mr. Bundeskanzler literally held it in the redout halls on April. I quote: “And therefore, for me and hopefully for all of us, it is clear that whoever impoverished the atrocities of the National Socialist regime and relativized the existence of camps, of gas chambers, does not fit into our institutional landscape.” – Quotes. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] What is more, you cannot express yourself on the question raised by you! Questions 4 to 8: I would like to argue, even though it will not be possible to investigate individual reasons from any distance of 60 years, that particularly in the final phase of the Second World War, deserteurs from the German Wehrmacht acted in the vast majority of legitimate motives. The fact that this is part of the basic consensus of the Second Republic also shows the legal measures adopted in the immediate post-war period, the so-called repeal and recruitment law: I mean the law of the 3rd century. ==References====External links==* Official website 48/1945, the Regulation of 5 December 1945, which was based on it. On September 6, 1945, as well as the so-called liberation amnesty, the Federal Law of 6 September 1945. March 1946 on the imposition of criminal proceedings, the forbearance of penalties and the eradication of convictions on the grounds of the liberation of Austria, Federal Law No. 79/1946, as amended by the Federal Act BGBl. No. 192/47. [[Abg. Dr. Glavischnig: Could you not distribute your manuscript? Then you wouldn't have to read it out!]] These laws, which are still in force today, have legally abolished the injustices against military deserators of the Nazi era and have legally rehabilitated the persons affected by these judgments. In this sense, it is also necessary to understand the legal structure represented by the Federal Ministry of Justice, which does not require additional legal provisions for the rehabilitation of Wehrmachtsdezeren. In this context, however, I also recall that the National Council has been dealing with this issue for several years and in this context also a study - also quoted in your request - which concludes with the following conclusion - I quote: "Since the Austrian legislator has already submitted a comprehensive regulation to the issue of the removal of judgments of the Nazi military justice, new legal measures are no longer necessary in accordance with the German NS Collection Act. Of course, this does not affect any political-moral acts for further processing this problem.” – Quotes. In this sense, I believe that it is important not to reduce this issue to a legal issue in the year of thought in 2005. The Federal Chancellor is therefore also in order to eliminate any doubt as to the correctness of the legal position described, with which Mr. President of the Federal Republic of Germany considers it appropriate to suspend judgments of the German war courts against desertors by a symbolic act of the legislator in the sense of an authentic interpretation. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] At the same time, of course, the victims of the Nazi military justice and their families should be respected by our Republic. As you know, this complex is currently being dealt with at the parliamentary level and, as I hear it, is on a good footing. I expressly support these efforts and declare my willingness to involve the expertise of the Federal Chancellery in these negotiations. On the question 9: The urgent request is based on a misinterpretation if it is claimed that a judicial decision is necessary in the sense of the Repeal and Injunction Act and the Liberation Directive to repeal judgments. Furthermore, I would like to point out that § 7 of the Liberation Recovery, as is reflected in the explanation of this law, is not expressly an amnesty provision. The legislator of liberation was not concerned with a collective mercy for the convicted, which was rightly rejected by the parties concerned, but with a clear sign of the demarcation of an injustice, on whose acts the resurgence of Austria was no longer bound in cases of military crimes. On the real question, I would like to point out that the issues raised in this urgent question are being discussed in depth in the judicial committee and I assume that an initiative is being set there, which clearly clarifyes the legal effects of those laws and, on the other hand, is a symbol of the recognition of the injustice of the Nazi military justice. Question 10: Following the legal basis of the Federal Ministry of Justice, all judgments of injustice were removed by the Repeal and Employment Act in 1945 and by the Liberation Council in 1946. In so far, the Austrian legal situation is clearly different from the German one, as is always known. Apart from the legal situation, I welcome recognition of the victims of the Nazi regime, including the victims of the Nazi military judgments. On the question 11: I refer to the answer to question 9, which I assume is that appropriate steps will be taken in the judicial committee for the purpose of the nature of the petitioners' intentions. Question 12: On this point I would like to refer to the legal executions in the study by Thomas Grünewald, which, in the course of his research, only encountered the largely unknown liberation of 1946. Among other things, Grünewald argues that, unlike the Enforcement and Termination Act, the liberation will be removed without exception and completely eliminate judgments of the military and SS courts and will therefore also cover those courts with condemned general crimes. On the question 13: As I have already pointed out, I see no compelling legal necessity to take legislative action beyond the existing laws, because Professor Moos himself writes that more clearly it is necessary for courts to have only the protocol function of certifying to the applicants that the judgment in question has been repealed by law. However, I am speaking for such an explanation and for a symbolic act of recognition of the victims of the Nazi military justice. Questions 14 to 16: To this end, I should like to point out that, in recent years, the official Austria has set out a number of measures in order to be responsible for the consequences of the time of National Socialism. I would like to mention the measures of the Art Restitution as well as the National and Reconciliation Fund and the General Remuneration Fund. In the sense of this very consistent political line of the Federal Government, I therefore believe that desertors from the German Wehrmacht, who for these reasons have become victims of the Nazi military justice, as well as their family members, must not suffer any social-law disadvantages. In the event of any further doubts, I agree that the Social Minister responsible for these issues should undergo such an examination and should, in the event of existing disadvantages, lead to the removal of such a requirement. Such a step could be taken without meddling in the parliamentary talks on this matter, probably also through a parliamentary resolution. Question 17: Yes. I will stand for the recognition of the homosexuals and the so-called asocial NS sacrifice, and in addition also the “force sterilised” you did not mention in the victim care law. [[It is a pleasure to the ÖVP and to representatives of the Freeman and the SPÖ.]] Questions 18 to 21: It is true that the VfGH has repealed in recognition G213/01, V62/01, individual provisions of the Volksgruppengesetz or the Topographieverordnung. The provisions were made in the 1970s, more precisely: 1976 and 1977. The Federal Chancellor then immediately began activities to establish a constitutional status and, in particular, accepted by all the parties concerned, taking into account the VfGH's statement. For a broad acceptance is the basic prerequisite for a successful living together of the peoples, as it also corresponds to the intention of the fathers of the State Treaty. The convening of the Consensus Conference was supported by the intention to establish this broad consensus between the representatives of the minority, the home associations and the country day parties. This first attempt was ultimately not successful in September 2002 due to the lack of the consent of a group representative. At the beginning of this year the talks were resumed. They run well and are accompanied by intensive consultations in Carinthia itself. As a concrete step, a direct information and discussion process should now take place in the municipalities. At the same time, as a sign of the good will of all the parties involved, it is planned to draw up in advance the local panels still missing in accordance with the 1977 Topography Regulation. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The rehabilitation of NS victims has been working on us since 1999. At that time, inspired by a German law, we also believed that we needed a law similar to that of the Federal Republic. After the study we commissioned – and this was clearly explained by Mr Stoisits – we were all surprised that there was another legal situation in us. The existing legal situation has been implemented by State Secretary Morak. According to the Enforcement and Termination Act 1945, convictions of Austrians for treasons and treasons of the high- and state or under the CPR are not deemed to have taken place if the actions against the national socialist rule or were aimed at restoring an independent state of Austria. These were the political judgments. At that time, all convictions made under taxatively cited laws were also removed. The Regulation to that effect extended the applicability of the repeal. It is true that a judgment of its own, which determines, is no longer necessary. As this differentiation between Amnesty and Rehabilitation has been repeatedly addressed today, I would like to point out once again that § 7 of the Liberation Recovery is not an amnesty provision. This has already been clearly explained by the historical legislator in the explanations. At the time, he has already argued that the legislator is not about an amnesty in the classical sense, not about a collective grace, but about a clear sign of the demarcation of an injustice, on whose acts Austria regained was no longer bound in the cases of the military offences. - I quoted here. In a law we should expressly point out this will of the historical legislator and, in an act here in Parliament, express respect and compassion for the victims of such judgments of injustice, for those in Austrian resistance, for the displaced and for their families. I invite all the political groups in this House to work together on a consensus here. I have already handed over our request to the opposition judges. We have time up to 1. June, where a judicial committee will take place. I am asking for the highest possible date of discussion, and I believe that we should not leave the consensus path that we have always pursued in the process of preparing history.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e17991	Madam President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! We reject this implementation of the bio-patent directives, not on principle, because we do not want to implement this directive, but because we do not agree with the way in which it is implemented. In 1998 the Biotechnology Directive was adopted at European level, while in the meantime there have been a number of technological developments – small things, for example, that the human genome has been deciphered. For seven years it has been possible to observe the impact of this Directive on the practice of patenting at the European Patent Office in Munich. And criticism on the basis of this practice – based on our rejection of this directive – is based on several points. One has seen in practice that this directive is not suitable and has great weaknesses. Some countries, as well as Austria, have been expecting the implementation of this Directive and have also made modifications on the basis of the analysis of the weaknesses and criticism of the practice of this Directive, such as Germany or France. I want to focus on two points now. There are a number of points that can be criticized. The first is that the terms “discovery”, “invention” and “pollutant protection” are mixed. On the basis of the short speaking time, a very simplified example. For example, if someone is able to isolate a substance, for example, by means of conventional inventions, the directive provides for the possibility of insulating or technically producing a gene or gene sequence from the human body, then he can obtain a patent if he can present an economic usefulness, even if this gene sequence or gene sequence, as they occur in nature, is identical. That's as if you're saying someone's developing a technique to get oil out of the earth, and then he doesn't have a patent on the technique of promoting the earth, but on the oil itself. And then this dust protection comes into play, which is an absolute protection of dust. That is, he also has a dust protection on the oil, i.e. not only on the recovery that he himself develops or discovers, such as the production of petrol or diesel, but also on any other production possibilities that anyone might come upon in the future, such as the production of Styrofoam or medications. This absolute protection of materials is a very big obstacle to research and development, but also to the economy. And here our criticism is that we are saying that we want a limited functional protection of substances, just as the other countries have already done. The second point concerns the possibility of patenting life and human genes. Practice has shown that hundreds of species, plants and human genes have already been patented at the European Patent Office. This means that the implementation of this Directive will result in this being possible. And we reject that. There are also examples of other countries that show that the directive can also be implemented in a limited manner, i.e. without these negative effects, which we consider to be anti-economic and research. That is why we are submitting a referral request which I would like to make. Request by Mr Krainer, Mr Grünewald, Members, on the reference to the report of the Economic Committee on the implementation of the Biotechnology Directive (615/921 d.b.) Once again to the Economic and Social Committee within the meaning of § 73 para. 3 Z 2 GG The signed Members put forward the request to complete the submission in the report of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy on the implementation of the Biotechnology Directive (615/921 d.b.) to refer the matter back to the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy. The reason for this is because, from our point of view, this draft law does not take sufficient account of the concerns I have previously expressed. I ask for the consent of the referral. Thank you.

It should no longer be a blind sample alone, but today's therapeutic possibilities must be reconciled with future therapeutic possibilities. It is not reasonable for people who cannot have themselves to be sentenced to worse treatment in double-blind studies, while there may be practical, quite common improvements and possibilities. I therefore believe that important concerns could be reached even after the comments of the Ethics Commission. We therefore support this project because we expect to recover from the Austrian research landscape. ==References====External links== ==References====External links== In the area of these research activities, there has been a lack of space. Point Report of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy on the proposal from the Commission to the Council (Doc._Other Organiser Federal Act establishing the Foreign Trade Act 2005 – AußHG 2005 and amending the War Material Act (923 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e19636	We now reach the 17th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The first speaker is Mr Magistrate. Hoscher. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e19668	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! A few words on the revision of the war material law. From our point of view, the proposed changes in key segments are insufficient. It is almost absurd, as the opinions also pointed out, that, as a result of the revision of relatively harmless weapons, much more precise and stricter legal provisions are applied in some cases than for heavy weapons according to the War Material Regulation. These low standards in the War Material Act concern, among other things, the specification of the authorisation criteria. In addition, we also believe that the criminal provisions give rise to criticism. In the War Material Act, these provisions must also be strengthened. Otherwise, for example, the physical export of a good could be punished differently from the electronic export of the software belonging to that good according to the War Material Regulation. We also believe that the Federal Government as the granting authority should be subject to similar criteria in the case of government operations, as they apply to private companies. The lack of transparency for us is particularly serious. The authorisation procedure is designed as a non-public single-party procedure. Appeals against decisions in this procedure can therefore only be taken by the applicant party. Thus, there are only legal remedies against refused authorisations, but not against authorisations which may have been wrongfully granted. Since the amendment tabled by the government parties does not really change the basics of these criticisms, we reject the amendment. Thank you.

At present, people with disabilities, those with care benefits, for example, who move their residence to another federal state, have to take a senseless and angry bureaucratic obstacle course. You must submit a new care benefit application either at the corresponding pension insurance or in the new federal state. Unfortunately, it happens many times here that care money classifications, which have been constant for years, because the disability has not been reduced, suddenly have no validity. People with disabilities then need not only to take the long way of calling, but also to fight for months to get their care allowance classification back. Ladies and gentlemen of the Government Party! Quit your non-social policy and support this demand so that the poorest in our country can finally really be helped.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e23756	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! In practice, the change of responsibilities for the granting of care allowances between the Land and the Federal Government, as set out in the motion for a resolution, occurs especially when a person in need of care who receives care from a country acquires a pension claim and thus falls within the competence of the Federal Government. In § 9 para. 1 of the Bundespflegegeldsgesetz was regulated that in these cases the care allowance should be due to the subsequent month of the absence of the country's capacity for benefits, which does not create a gap in relation to it, Mrs. Haidlmayr. In fact, the problem lies in the fact that changing to the federal government as a paying-out place of the care money can lead to a reclassification and thus to a change in financial services. As an examination of our ministry showed, about 1,200 such facts occur each year. It is especially noteworthy in this context that 62 percent, almost two thirds of cases, do not change. In almost a quarter – I correct you – at 22 percent exactly – not 20 percent, as you have said, there is even a better assessment by the federal government. [[Abg. Haidlmayr: And the difference? I miss the difference!]] It should also be noted, Mrs Haidlmayr, that these changes are due to time, and can be caused by deterioration, but also by improvements in health conditions and thus care needs. Either the state of health improves or it worsens. [[Abg. Haidlmayr: Many are worse off than before!]] At the beginning of the care allowance system, such differences were more common and also much more relevant from the dimension, since you are already right, but through the extensive jurisdiction of the Supreme Court and also due to clear guidelines and occasions, however, there has been a harmonisation of the whole system. Essentially for this development was also a federal-state meeting in April of the previous year, in April 2003. It was agreed that the social security institutions should take over the classification made by the country and, if the opinion is to be considered coherent and comprehensible, no own assessment should be carried out. Only if, in the circumstances of the individual case, in particular from the content of the file, evidence indicates that the classification will change, the facts must be raised on their own initiative. There is no path of suffering for the caregiver, as you have mentioned it, but the examination is carried out in the course of a timely house visit by the official. [[Abg. Haidlmayr: It won't be announced!]] We therefore consider a new regulation as not necessary. In some cases, she would even have an impact on the health care worker or the care worker. Mrs Haidlmayr, I can also give you a letter here, which was sent to the main association two years ago by our ministry, and as far as I know, the social security institutions also adhere to it.

The result of these events is that many sporting talents have already crystallized, that the students are motivated and encouraged by trophies and documents to engage in sports. But there is also another reason why movement at any age should be important and, in fact, self-evident: our own health, for which we are responsible. This alone should give us enough reason to give school sports an important priority and not to shorten hours, as the Federal Minister did two years ago. Let's think about it! Our health and those of our children are the most important.

ParlaMint-AT_2005-05-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00110_d7e25356	Mr President. Mrs. Minister! I'm talking about the media law. Ladies and gentlemen of my group, who are speaking in my opinion, will devote themselves to the other legislative proposals you have referred to. The Media Act protects against violation of the highest personal area of life, from breach of the presumption of innocence, and provides protection against prohibited reporting. [[Abg. Parnigoni: It does not protect against the bowl screen!]] With today's novelle, this protection now also applies to content that is transmitted electronically. Electronic content, broadcast by radio, for example via websites, is now also subject to media law, and we have adapted the instruments to these electronic media. For example, if the Internet violates the presumption of innocence, the aim of the new regulation is to remove the incriminating statements of third parties, for example in chats, from the website as soon as possible. We need new instruments for this, of course, because the classical confiscation or confiscation does not take place here. However, the strict rules for localisation and subsequent communications apply only to websites that have information beyond the presentation of the personal area of life and the presentation of the media holder and are therefore appropriate to influence public opinion. That means we distinguish between large and small websites. This protection is necessary and is also applicable internationally, and, of course, we can only access domestic providers in international declarations. Legal protection is something that should be comprehensive and not only available in print media.

I would also like to thank the Minister for Justice, who is particularly willing to take account of the needs of the economy. I am pleased that we can adopt this important law together with the opposition today. Thank you.
